Building and rendering 3D worlds from images 110

Making 3D reconstructions and renderings from photos and video, often using neural approaches that learn how light interacts with scenes.

Ultra-fast 3D worlds from camera images

75

Creating detailed 3D scenes from photos in seconds rather than hours, using splatting techniques to avoid slow geometry solving.

ICLR 2026

UltraGauss: Ultrafast Gaussian Reconstruction of 3D Ultrasound Volumes

Problem. 2D ultrasound interpretation is highly operator-dependent and cognitively demanding, hindering reproducibility and reliability of clinical diagnosis.
Approach. We present UltraGauss, an ultrasound-specific Gaussian Splatting framework that renders via probe-plane intersection with in-plane aggregation, aligning with plane-based echo sampling.
Contribution. UltraGauss delivers state-of-the-art 2D-to-3D reconstructions reaching 0.99 SSIM within approximately 20 minutes on a single GPU, with clinical expert surveys rating reconstructions the most realistic.
gaussian splattingultrasound reconstructionprobe-plane intersection3d volumetric imaging

ICLR 2026

ReSplat: Degradation-agnostic Feed-forward Gaussian Splatting via Self-guided Residual Diffusion

Problem. Novel view synthesis typically assumes ideal, clear inputs and struggles under real-world image degradations like blur, low-light, haze, rain, and snow.
Approach. ReSplat jointly estimates restored images and gaussians for clear scene representation, using 3D gaussians from diffusion sampling as self-guidance for multi-view consistent image restoration.
Contribution. ReSplat significantly outperforms existing methods across blur, low-light, haze, rain, and snow conditions, delivering superior visual quality for robust NVS.
image restorationgaussian splattingmulti-view consistency
ICLR 2026

3DGEER: 3D Gaussian Rendering Made Exact and Efficient for Generic Cameras

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting relies on 2D projections which degrade accuracy under generic large field-of-view cameras.
Approach. Introduces 3DGEER with closed-form ray-Gaussian integral expressions, Particle Bounding Frustum for efficient association, and Bipolar Equiangular Projection.
Contribution. Outperforms prior methods across all metrics, runs 5x faster than projective-exact baselines, and generalizes to wider fields-of-view unseen during training.
gaussian splattingrenderingcamera models
ICLR 2026

Streaming Visual Geometry Transformer

Problem. Low-latency 3D reconstruction from videos requires online processing but existing methods are designed for offline use.
Approach. Proposes streaming visual geometry transformer using causal attention with cached keys/values and knowledge distillation from bidirectional model.
Contribution. Enhances inference speed in online scenarios while maintaining competitive performance for scalable interactive 3D vision.
transformers3d reconstructionknowledge distillation
ICLR 2026

Gradient-Direction-Aware Density Control for 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting suffers from over-reconstruction where large Gaussians cannot split due to conflicting gradients, and over-densification in regions with aligned gradient aggregation.
Approach. GDAGS introduces Gradient Coherence Ratio to discriminate Gaussians by gradient direction alignment and applies gradient-direction-aware density control for splitting and cloning.
Contribution. Reduces memory overhead and improves geometric detail reconstruction across real-world benchmarks by prioritizing gradient-direction sensitive splitting operations.
3D Gaussian Splattingdensity controlgradient analysis
ICLR 2026

SSD-GS: Scattering and Shadow Decomposition for Relightable 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Existing 3DGS relighting methods use coarse shading decompositions, limiting fidelity and physical interpretability for anisotropic metals and translucent materials.
Approach. Decompose reflectance into four components (diffuse, specular, shadow, subsurface scattering) with learnable dipole-based scattering, occlusion-aware shadows, and anisotropic Fresnel-based specular.
Contribution. SSD-GS achieves superior relighting quality on OLAT datasets while enabling controllable light editing and interactive scene relighting with improved physical interpretability.
physically-based rendering3d gaussian splattingsubsurface scatteringrelighting

ICLR 2026

D$^2$GS: Depth-and-Density Guided Gaussian Splatting for Stable and Accurate Sparse-View Reconstruction

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting degrades and becomes unstable under sparse-view conditions due to overfitting near camera and underfitting in distant areas.
Approach. Introduce depth-and-density guided dropout to suppress overfitting and distance-aware fidelity enhancement for under-fitted regions, propose stability metric for robustness.
Contribution. D²GS significantly improves visual quality and robustness in sparse-view conditions across multiple datasets while providing stability measurement insights.
3d gaussian splattingsparse-view reconstructionadaptive dropoutstability metrics

ICLR 2026

Uncertainty-Aware 3D Reconstruction for Dynamic Underwater Scenes

Problem. Underwater 3D reconstruction is challenging due to light scattering, environment dynamics, and sensitivity to observation noise.
Approach. UDF jointly represents underwater structure and view-dependent medium over time using 3D Gaussians in a volumetric medium field with deformation and medium offset networks.
Contribution. The method achieves high-quality reconstruction and novel view synthesis while addressing input-dependent noise through uncertainty modeling.
3d gaussian splattinguncertainty estimationdynamic fields
ICLR 2026

Mono4DGS-HDR: High Dynamic Range 4D Gaussian Splatting from Alternating-exposure Monocular Videos

Problem. Reconstructing high dynamic range 4D scenes from unposed monocular low dynamic range videos captured with alternating exposures is unsolved and presents significant challenges.
Approach. Mono4DGS-HDR uses a two-stage Gaussian Splatting approach: first learning video HDR Gaussians in orthographic camera space without pose requirements, then transforming to world space and refining jointly with camera poses using temporal luminance regularization.
Contribution. Mono4DGS-HDR is the first system for 4D HDR reconstruction from alternating-exposure monocular videos and significantly outperforms adapted baselines in rendering quality and speed.
gaussian splattinghdr reconstructiontemporal consistency
ICLR 2026

Splat and Distill: Augmenting Teachers with Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction For 3D-Aware Distillation

Problem. Vision Foundation Models lack 3D awareness despite their effectiveness in 2D tasks, limiting their application to geometry-dependent downstream tasks.
Approach. Splat and Distill augments teacher VFMs with a fast, feed-forward 3D Gaussian lifting pipeline that splatts features onto novel viewpoints to create geometrically grounded supervision for student models.
Contribution. Our method significantly outperforms prior work on depth estimation, normal prediction, and correspondence tasks, improving 3D awareness while enhancing semantic richness of 2D features.
gaussian splattingknowledge distillation3d reconstruction
ICLR 2026

Signal Structure-Aware Gaussian Splatting for Large-Scale Scene Reconstruction

Problem. Large-scale 3D Gaussian Splatting struggles with sparsely observed regions that have few initial points, leading to uncontrolled densification, redundant primitives, and poor efficiency.
Approach. SIG reframes scene reconstruction as signal structure recovery, deriving average sampling frequency and bandwidth to regulate training image resolution and Gaussian densification based on scene frequency convergence, and introduces Sphere-Constrained Gaussians for geometry-aware optimization.
Contribution. SIG achieves state-of-the-art performance in large-scale scene reconstruction with substantially improved efficiency and rendering quality without floater artifacts.
signal structure recoveryfrequency-aware schedulingsphere-constrained gaussiansadaptive densification

ICLR 2026

Open-Set Semantic Gaussian Splatting SLAM with Expandable Representation

Problem. Capturing open-ended 3D scenes with rich semantics on everyday devices is challenging due to unscalable semantic integration, prohibitive memory costs, and cross-view inconsistency.
Approach. Open-Set Semantic GS-SLAM uses an expandable semantic feature pool decoupled from individual 3D Gaussians with consistency-aware optimization and Semantic Stability Guidance.
Contribution. Achieves high-fidelity rendering with scalable, open-set semantics across controlled and in-the-wild environments, supporting applications like 3D localization and scene editing.
gaussian splatting slamsemantic feature poolingconsistency-aware optimization
ICLR 2026

FastGHA: Generalized Few-Shot 3D Gaussian Head Avatars with Real-Time Animation

Problem. Efficiently generating high-fidelity 3D Gaussian head avatars from few images with real-time animation remains challenging due to scalability and per-identity optimization requirements.
Approach. FastGHA is a feed-forward method that learns per-pixel Gaussian representations using transformer-based encoder fusing DINOv3 and Stable Diffusion features, with lightweight MLP-based dynamic network.
Contribution. Significantly outperforms existing methods in rendering quality and inference efficiency while supporting real-time dynamic avatar animation from few input images.
3d gaussian splattingfeed-forward avatar generationdynamic deformation network
ICLR 2026

A^2TG: Adaptive Anisotropic Textured Gaussians for Efficient 3D Scene Representation

Problem. Fixed square textures in textured Gaussian Splatting methods lead to inefficient memory usage and limited adaptability to varying scene complexity.
Approach. A^2TG equips each Gaussian primitive with an anisotropic texture and uses gradient-guided adaptation to jointly determine texture resolution and aspect ratio in a detail-aware manner.
Contribution. A^2TG achieves comparable rendering fidelity to fixed-texture methods while substantially reducing memory requirements across multiple benchmark datasets.
gaussian splattinganisotropic texturesadaptive resolution
ICLR 2026

Distractor-free Generalizable 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Generalizable 3D Gaussian Splatting struggles with distractor impacts in training and inference phases, leading to training instability and artifacts.
Approach. DGGS proposes distractor-free generalizable training via feed-forward mask prediction and refinement, and two-stage inference with reference scoring and distractor pruning to remove residual distractor influences.
Contribution. DGGS addresses distractor-free generalization and demonstrates superior reconstruction capability on novel distractor scenes.
3D gaussian splattingdistractor removalmask predictionfeed-forward inference

ICLR 2026

YoNoSplat: You Only Need One Model for Feedforward 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Fast 3D scene reconstruction from unstructured image collections with arbitrary numbers of images, variable camera calibration, and camera pose estimation remains challenging.
Approach. YoNoSplat is a feedforward model that predicts local Gaussians and camera poses for each view, aggregated into a global representation via a novel mixing training strategy that gradually transitions from ground-truth to predicted poses and embeds camera intrinsics into the network.
Contribution. YoNoSplat achieves state-of-the-art performance on standard benchmarks in both pose-free and pose-dependent settings while reconstructing 100 views at 280×518 resolution in 2.69 seconds on an NVIDIA GH200 GPU.
3D gaussian splattingfeedforward reconstructioncamera pose estimationmixing training strategy

ICLR 2026

Stylos: Multi-View 3D Stylization with Single-Forward Gaussian Splatting

Problem. 3D style transfer on complex scenes requires per-scene optimization and precomputed poses, limiting scalability and applicability to diverse unposed inputs.
Approach. Stylos adopts a Transformer backbone with two pathways where geometry predictions retain self-attention while style is injected via global cross-attention, combined with a voxel-based 3D style loss to align aggregated scene features to style statistics.
Contribution. Stylos achieves high-quality zero-shot style transfer on single-view and large-scale multi-view settings without per-scene optimization or precomputed poses while generalizing to unseen categories and styles.
3D gaussian splattingstyle losscross-attentionzero-shot stylization

ICLR 2026

Augmented Radiance Field: A General Framework for Enhanced Gaussian Splatting

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting's reliance on spherical harmonics for color encoding limits its ability to separate diffuse and specular components, making complex reflections difficult to represent accurately.
Approach. An augmented Gaussian kernel explicitly models specular effects through view-dependent opacity and uses an error-driven compensation strategy to improve rendering quality through adaptive insertion and optimization.
Contribution. The method surpasses state-of-the-art NeRF methods in rendering performance while achieving greater parameter efficiency.
3D gaussian splattingspecular modelingview-dependent opacity
ICLR 2026

Variation-aware Flexible 3D Gaussian Editing

Problem. Indirect 2D-to-3D editing methods for Gaussian Splatting introduce cross-view inconsistencies and constrain editing flexibility and efficiency.
Approach. Proposes VF-Editor, which predicts attribute variations for Gaussian primitives directly in 3D using a novel variation predictor distilled from 2D editing knowledge and parallel decoding functions.
Contribution. Enables flexible knowledge transfer from diverse 2D editors into a single unified 3D predictor, revealing limitations of indirect pipelines and validating native 3D editing effectiveness.
3d gaussian splattingvariation predictionknowledge distillationattribute editing
ICLR 2026

UniSplat: Unified Spatio-Temporal Fusion via 3D Latent Scaffolds for Dynamic Driving Scene Reconstruction

Problem. Feed-forward 3D reconstruction for autonomous driving struggles with sparse, non-overlapping camera views and complex scene dynamics.
Approach. Present UniSplat, a framework constructing 3D latent scaffolds from foundation models, with efficient spatio-temporal fusion mechanism operating within scaffolds, and dual-branch decoder generating dynamic-aware Gaussians plus static memory.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art novel view synthesis with robust renderings even for viewpoints outside original camera coverage.
3D latent scaffoldsspatio-temporal fusiongaussian splattingdual-branch decoder
ICLR 2026

WinT3R: Window-Based Streaming Reconstruction with Camera Token Pool

Problem. Online 3D reconstruction from streaming video faces trade-off between reconstruction quality and real-time performance with uncertain camera poses.
Approach. WinT3R uses sliding window for sufficient frame information exchange and maintains global camera token pool with compact camera representation for reliable pose estimation.
Contribution. State-of-the-art online reconstruction quality, camera pose estimation, and speed validated on diverse datasets.
3d reconstructionstreamingcamera pose estimationfeed-forward models
ICLR 2026

Beyond Visual Reconstruction Quality: Object Perception-aware 3D Gaussian Splatting for Autonomous Driving

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting for autonomous driving prioritizes visual similarity but fails to preserve perception module behavior, undermining ADS applicability.
Approach. Adds perception-aligned loss leveraging output differences and object zone quality loss reinforcing training on perception-model-identified object locations.
Contribution. Improved ability of reconstructed scenes to preserve perception module output consistency with ground-truth images.
3d gaussian splattingperception-awareautonomous drivingobject detection
ICLR 2026

The Less You Depend, The More You Learn: Synthesizing Novel Views from Sparse, Unposed Images without Any 3D Knowledge

Problem. Novel view synthesis methods diverge between 3D knowledge-driven approaches and data-centric methods; it remains unclear which paradigm is more scalable with increasing data.
Approach. Feed-forward NVS framework removes explicit scene structure and pose annotation reliance, learning implicit 3D awareness directly from vast 2D images without pose information.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art NVS performance even outperforming methods relying on posed training data, validating data-centric scalability principle.
novel view synthesisfeed-forward generationimplicit 3d learningdata-centric training
ICLR 2026

ARTDECO: Toward High-Fidelity On-the-Fly Reconstruction with Hierarchical Gaussian Structure and Feed-Forward Guidance

Problem. On-the-fly 3D reconstruction from monocular images faces a major trade-off: per-scene optimization yields high fidelity but is computationally expensive, while feed-forward models enable real-time inference but struggle with accuracy.
Approach. ARTDECO combines 3D foundation models for pose and point prediction with a Gaussian decoder and hierarchical Gaussian representation with LoD-aware rendering strategy.
Contribution. ARTDECO delivers interactive performance comparable to SLAM, robustness similar to feed-forward systems, and reconstruction quality close to per-scene optimization across eight diverse benchmarks.
gaussian splattinghierarchical representationfoundation models
ICLR 2026

Less Gaussians, Texture More: 4K Feed-Forward Textured Splatting

Problem. Existing feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting methods require pixel-aligned primitives, making 4K resolution prohibitive as the number of Gaussians grows quadratically with image resolution.
Approach. Predicts compact geometric primitives and associated per-primitive texture maps in a single forward pass without per-scene optimization, replacing reliance on pixel-aligned Gaussians.
Contribution. Enables feed-forward 4K 3D Gaussian Splatting without quadratic scaling in Gaussians.
3d gaussian splattingtexture mappingfeed-forward generation
ICLR 2026

STream3R: Scalable Sequential 3D Reconstruction with Causal Transformer

Problem. Multi-view 3D reconstruction either requires expensive global optimization or relies on simplistic memory mechanisms that scale poorly with sequence length.
Approach. Reformulates pointmap prediction as decoder-only Transformer problem using streaming framework with causal attention to process image sequences efficiently, learning geometric priors from large-scale data.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms prior work on static and dynamic scene benchmarks while being compatible with LLM-style training infrastructure for efficient large-scale pretraining.
3d reconstructiontransformer architecturescausal attentionstreaming models
ICLR 2026

Pixel to Gaussian: Ultra-Fast Continuous Super-Resolution with 2D Gaussian Modeling

Problem. Arbitrary-scale image super-resolution requires repeated upsampling and decoding, limiting efficiency and reconstruction fidelity.
Approach. We propose ContinuousSR with Pixel-to-Gaussian paradigm using 2D Gaussian Splatting, introducing Deep Gaussian Prior-driven covariance weighting and Adaptive Position Drifting.
Contribution. ContinuousSR enables 1ms arbitrary-scale rendering with significant improvements on seven benchmarks.
gaussian splattingcontinuous super-resolutionadaptive weighting
ICLR 2026

StreamSplat: Towards Online Dynamic 3D Reconstruction from Uncalibrated Video Streams

Problem. Real-time reconstruction of dynamic 3D scenes from uncalibrated video requires robust online methods under latency and memory constraints, but most methods require hours of optimization with full-sequence access.
Approach. We introduce StreamSplat, a feed-forward framework with probabilistic Gaussian prediction, bidirectional deformation fields for frame association, and adaptive Gaussian fusion.
Contribution. StreamSplat achieves state-of-the-art quality with 1200x speedup over optimization methods while supporting arbitrary-length online reconstruction.
gaussian splattingonline reconstructionfeed-forward generation
ICLR 2026

Frequency-aware Dynamic Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Dynamic Gaussian Splatting methods struggle to balance high-frequency rendering details with high-frequency motion, causing motion blur in 4D novel view synthesis.
Approach. Introduce frequency-differentiated Gaussian kernels for adaptive alpha-blending and Fourier-Deformation Network using high-frequency embeddings to capture diverse motion patterns.
Contribution. FAGS significantly reduces motion blur and enhances structural details, achieving state-of-the-art 4D reconstruction performance on synthetic and real-world benchmarks.
gaussian splattingfrequency analysisfourier embeddings
ICLR 2026

MoE-GS: Mixture of Experts for Dynamic Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Dynamic scene reconstruction with 3D Gaussian Splatting shows inconsistent performance across diverse scenes, indicating no single approach handles all dynamic challenges effectively.
Approach. MoE-GS combines multiple specialized experts through a novel Volume-aware Pixel Router that adaptively blends expert outputs by projecting volumetric Gaussian-level weights into pixel space via differentiable weight splatting.
Contribution. Demonstrates consistent improvements in rendering quality on N3V and Technicolor datasets while addressing efficiency through single-pass rendering, gate-aware pruning, and distillation to individual experts.
mixture of expertsgaussian splattingdynamic scene reconstructionneural rendering
ICLR 2026

ODE-GS: Latent ODEs for Dynamic Scene Extrapolation with 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Dynamic scene reconstruction methods rely on time-conditioned deformation networks limited to interpolation within fixed time windows preventing future extrapolation.
Approach. ODE-GS integrates 3D Gaussian Splatting with latent neural ODEs modeling Gaussian parameter trajectories as continuous-time dynamics enabling arbitrary future timestamp rendering.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art extrapolation improving metrics by 19.8% compared to leading baselines on D-NeRF, NVFi, and HyperNeRF.
gaussian splattingneural odescontinuous dynamics
ICLR 2026

VoMP: Predicting Volumetric Mechanical Property Fields

Problem. Physical simulation requires spatially-varying mechanical properties that are typically laboriously hand-crafted, limiting automation in content creation and scientific applications.
Approach. VoMP is a feed-forward model predicting Young's modulus, Poisson's ratio, and density throughout object volumes by aggregating multi-view features and mapping to a trained material manifold.
Contribution. VoMP estimates accurate volumetric properties enabling simulation-ready asset conversion with realistic deformable simulations.
3d reconstructionvision transformersmaterial estimationphysical simulation
ICLR 2026

MEGS^{2}: Memory-Efficient Gaussian Splatting via Spherical Gaussians and Unified Pruning

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting has high memory consumption limiting applicability on edge devices; existing compression methods focus on storage but fail to address critical rendering memory bottleneck.
Approach. MEGS² replaces memory-intensive Spherical Harmonics with lightweight, arbitrarily oriented and prunable Spherical Gaussian lobes, proposing a unified soft pruning framework modeling primitive and lobe pruning as constrained optimization.
Contribution. MEGS² achieves 50% static VRAM reduction and 40% rendering VRAM reduction compared to existing methods while maintaining comparable rendering quality.
3d gaussian splattingspherical gaussianspruningmemory optimization
ICLR 2026

From Tokens to Nodes: Semantic-Guided Motion Control for Dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Dynamic 3D reconstruction from monocular videos suffers from ambiguous 3D motion inference and high computational demands due to millions of Gaussians.
Approach. The framework uses semantic and motion priors to establish patch-token-node correspondences and applies motion-adaptive compression to concentrate control points in dynamic regions.
Contribution. The approach achieves significant improvements in reconstruction quality and efficiency over state-of-the-art methods through adaptive control point allocation.
motion-adaptive compressiondynamic gaussian splattingspline-based trajectory
ICLR 2026

One2Scene: Geometric Consistent Explorable 3D Scene Generation from a Single Image

Problem. Single-image 3D scene generation suffers from geometric distortions and noise when viewpoint moves far from original perspective, limiting true explorable generation.
Approach. One2Scene decomposes the task: panorama generation for initialization, feed-forward Gaussian Splatting lift to 3D scaffold via multi-view stereo matching with cross-view consistency, then novel view synthesis.
Contribution. Substantially outperforms state-of-the-art on panorama depth estimation, 360° reconstruction, and supports immersive scene exploration with geometrically accurate large viewpoint motions.
gaussian splattingmulti-view stereo3d reconstruction
ICLR 2026

CylinderSplat: 3D Gaussian Splatting with Cylindrical Triplanes for Panoramic Novel View Synthesis

Problem. Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting struggles with panoramic imagery due to multi-view cost volume limitations and poor geometric capture with Cartesian Triplanes.
Approach. CylinderSplat uses cylindrical Triplane representation better aligned with 360° scenes and a dual-branch architecture for pixel-based and volume-based reconstruction.
Contribution. CylinderSplat achieves state-of-the-art results in single-view and multi-view panoramic novel view synthesis with improved reconstruction and geometric accuracy.
3d gaussian splattingpanoramic synthesiscylindrical representation
ICLR 2026

Uncertainty Matters in Dynamic Gaussian Splatting for Monocular 4D Reconstruction

Problem. Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes from monocular input is fundamentally under-constrained, causing motion drifts under occlusion and degraded synthesis at novel views.
Approach. USplat4D estimates time-varying per-Gaussian uncertainty and leverages it to construct a spatio-temporal graph for uncertainty-aware optimization of Gaussian primitives.
Contribution. USplat4D consistently improves dynamic Gaussian Splatting models, yielding stable geometry under occlusion and high-quality synthesis at extreme viewpoints.
gaussian splattinguncertainty estimationmonocular reconstruction

Progressive Gaussian Transformer with Anisotropy-aware Sampling for Open Vocabulary Occupancy Prediction

Problem. 3D occupancy prediction trades off between sparse Gaussian representations struggling with small objects and dense representations with high computational overhead.
Approach. PG-Occ employs progressive online densification for gradually enhancing 3D Gaussian representation and anisotropy-aware sampling with spatio-temporal fusion for adaptive receptive fields.
Contribution. PG-Occ achieves state-of-the-art with 14.3% mIoU improvement over previous best method on open-vocabulary occupancy prediction.

Flash-Mono: Feed-Forward Accelerated Gaussian Splatting Monocular SLAM

Problem. Monocular 3D Gaussian Splatting SLAM suffers from time-consuming per-frame optimization, geometric inaccuracy, and multi-view inconsistency.
Approach. Present Flash-Mono using feed-forward prediction to directly forecast camera poses and Gaussian properties from multi-frame context via recurrent cross-attention, with 2D Gaussian surfels replacing 3D ellipsoids.
Contribution. Achieves 10× speedup compared to optimization-based methods while ensuring high-quality rendering and robust loop closure via hidden-state submap descriptors.

SurfSplat: Conquering Feedforward 2D Gaussian Splatting with Surface Continuity Priors

Problem. Feedforward 3D reconstruction from sparse images often produces discrete, color-biased point clouds lacking surface continuity.
Approach. Present SurfSplat using 2D Gaussian Splatting with surface continuity priors and forced alpha blending, introducing High-Resolution Rendering Consistency (HRRC) for evaluation.
Contribution. Reconstructs coherent geometry with faithful textures outperforming prior methods on RealEstate10K, DL3DV, and ScanNet.

ICLR 2026

UP2You: Fast Reconstruction of Yourself from Unconstrained Photo Collections

Problem. Reconstructing high-fidelity 3D clothed portraits from unconstrained in-the-wild photos varies significantly in pose, viewpoint, cropping, and occlusion.
Approach. Introduce UP2You, a tuning-free solution using data rectifier paradigm to convert unconstrained inputs into clean orthogonal multi-view images, with pose-correlated feature aggregation (PCFA) module.
Contribution. Surpasses prior methods in geometric accuracy (15% Chamfer reduction, 18% P2S on PuzzleIOI) and texture fidelity; efficient at 1.5 minutes per person with multi-garment virtual try-on support.

ICLR 2026

A Step to Decouple Optimization in 3DGS

Problem. 3DGS optimization suffers from overlooked details: update step coupling inducing optimizer state rescaling and gradient coupling in moments leading to under/over-effective regularization.
Approach. The work decouples 3DGS optimization into Sparse Adam, Re-State Regularization, and Decoupled Attribute Regularization, re-coupling beneficial components into AdamW-GS.
Contribution. AdamW-GS achieves better optimization efficiency and representation effectiveness simultaneously under 3DGS and 3DGS-MCMC frameworks.
sparse adamdecoupled regularization3d gaussian splatting
ICLR 2026

SimULi: Real-Time LiDAR and Camera Simulation with Unscented Transforms

Problem. Existing neural rendering methods achieve high fidelity but suffer from low rendering speeds or only support pinhole camera models, lacking support for complex lenses and LiDAR data needed for autonomous driving simulation.
Approach. Proposes SimULi, extending 3DGS with LiDAR support via automated tiling for arbitrary spinning LiDARs, ray-based culling, and factorized Gaussian representation for cross-sensor consistency.
Contribution. Renders 10-20x faster than ray tracing and 1.5-14x faster than prior rasterization while matching or exceeding fidelity, handling wide camera model range on autonomous driving datasets.
3D Gaussian splattingLiDAR simulationray cullingmulti-sensor rendering
ICLR 2026

DiffWind: Physics-Informed Differentiable Modeling of Wind-Driven Object Dynamics

Problem. Modeling wind-driven object dynamics from video is challenging because wind is invisible and varies spatio-temporally, and objects undergo complex deformations.
Approach. Represents wind as a grid-based physical field and objects as particle systems using 3D Gaussian Splatting and Material Point Method, with Lattice Boltzmann physics constraints.
Contribution. Significantly outperforms prior dynamic scene modeling in both reconstruction accuracy and simulation fidelity, enabling novel wind retargeting applications.
material point methodlattice boltzmann methodgaussian splattingdifferentiable rendering
ICLR 2026

ComGS: Efficient 3D Object-Scene Composition via Surface Octahedral Probes

Problem. 3D object-scene composition with Gaussian Splatting suffers from baked appearance preventing realistic composition and requires expensive ray-tracing for relightable reconstruction.
Approach. ComGS introduces Surface Octahedral Probes storing lighting/occlusion information for efficient querying via interpolation, and simplifies lighting by focusing on environment at object placement.
Contribution. Achieves high-quality real-time rendering at ~26 FPS with at least 2× speedup in reconstruction, producing visually harmonious compositions through efficient inverse rendering.
3d renderinggaussian splattinginverse rendering
ICLR 2026

NGS-Marker: Robust Native Watermarking for 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting models are vulnerable to partial infringement attacks where adversaries extract and reuse subsets of Gaussians, and existing watermarking protects only rendered images.
Approach. NGS-Marker integrates jointly-trained watermark injector and decoder with gradient-based progressive injection for full-scene coverage, enabling robust ownership decoding from local regions.
Contribution. Effectively defends against partial infringement while supporting hybrid protection and multimodal watermarking for practical real-world deployment.

Sharp Monocular View Synthesis in Less Than a Second

Problem. Single-image view synthesis requires expensive optimization or per-image training, limiting real-time photorealistic synthesis across camera movements.
Approach. SHARP regresses 3D Gaussian representation parameters from a single image via single feedforward neural network pass, enabling metric, zero-shot view synthesis.
Contribution. Reduces LPIPS by 25-34% and DISTS by 21-43% versus best prior model, achieving photorealistic synthesis in less than a second with three orders of magnitude speed improvement.

ICLR 2026

Active Learning of 3D Gaussian Splatting with Consistent Region Partition and Robust Pose Estimation

Problem. Collecting informative images for 3D Gaussian Splatting reconstruction lacks feedback, causing inefficient and potentially deficient data gathering.
Approach. Active learning algorithm estimates most informative image poses by partitioning consistent regions via Gaussian attributes, evaluating semantic feature variance per Gaussian, and optimizing noisy poses.
Contribution. Demonstrates robust active learning performance under accurate and noisy pose conditions on both synthetic and real-world scenes.

ICLR 2026

Fracture-GS: Dynamic Fracture Simulation with Physics-Integrated Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Existing methods fail to capture the complex physics of extreme collisions, producing non-physical artifacts and material adhesion at fracture interfaces.
Approach. Fracture-GS enhances Collision Material Point Method with momentum-conserving interface forces and integrates it with fracture-aware 3D Gaussian continuum representation through implicit reconstruction, collision resolution, and dynamic fracture tracking.
Contribution. The framework demonstrates superior physical accuracy and computational efficiency in handling homogeneous materials, heterogeneous composites, and multi-body collisions compared to existing methods.
material point methodgaussian splattingphysics simulation3d reconstruction
ICLR 2026

CLoD-GS: Continuous Level-of-Detail via 3D Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Discrete Level-of-Detail rendering requires significant storage for model copies and causes jarring visual popping artifacts during transitions.
Approach. CLoD-GS introduces a continuous LoD mechanism directly into 3D Gaussian Splatting with learnable distance-dependent decay parameters that adjust Gaussian opacity based on viewpoint proximity.
Contribution. CLoD-GS enables smooth quality scaling within a single unified model while reducing primitive count and memory footprint compared to discrete methods.
gaussian splattinglevel of detail3d renderingneural representations
ICLR 2026

FieryGS: In-the-Wild Fire Synthesis with Physics-Integrated Gaussian Splatting

Problem. Traditional CFD and graphics pipelines for fire effects require handcrafted geometry and expert-tuned parameters, limiting scalability to real-world scenes.
Approach. FieryGS integrates multimodal LLM-based material reasoning, efficient volumetric combustion simulation, and unified rendering of fire and 3DGS through three modules: implicit reconstruction, combustion simulation, and dynamic fracture tracking.
Contribution. FieryGS enables realistic, controllable fire synthesis in real scenes with precise user control over intensity, airflow, ignition location, and other combustion parameters.
gaussian splattingphysics simulationllm-based reasoningfire rendering
ICML 2026

Trust It or Not: Evidential Uncertainty for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction with Trust3R

Problem. Feed-forward 3D reconstruction produces heuristic confidence scores lacking probabilistic grounding, failing to indicate where and how much predicted geometry can be trusted.
Approach. Trust3R combines gated residual mean refinement with Normal-Inverse-Wishart evidential head to yield multivariate Student-t distribution for per-point uncertainty.
Contribution. Achieves 25% lower AURC and 41% lower AUSE on ScanNet++; improves risk-coverage and sparsification across diverse indoor/outdoor benchmarks.
evidential uncertaintystudent-t distributiongeometric uncertainty

Expo-GS: Exposure-Aware Signed Distance Function in Gaussian Splatting for High Dynamic Range

Problem. High dynamic range novel view synthesis suffers from geometric artifacts and radiometric distortions under multi-exposure conditions due to exposure and structure not being jointly addressed.
Approach. Decompose HDR-NVS into Irradiance and Geometry fields with exposure-aware signed distance function that reweights supervision by local exposure reliability.
Contribution. Expo-GS achieves peak PSNR of 39.06 dB under HDR and 41.38 dB in LDR-OE, preserving high-frequency textures and maintaining structural consistency.

Contribution. Achieves superior surface reconstruction performance across challenging scenarios while maintaining broad compatibility.

PCGS: Deblurring 3D Gaussian Splatting with Patch Comparison

Problem. 3D Gaussian Splatting encounters challenges in regions with overlapping Gaussians, leading to blurring and artifacts due to insufficient positional gradients for handling such circumstances.
Approach. PCGS adaptively controls Gaussian densification by dividing images into patches, identifying error-prone regions by comparing rendered and ground truth images, and applying a Gaussian control strategy with importance-based sampling.
Contribution. Achieves significantly fewer artifacts and less blur while maintaining a Gaussian count approximately equal to vanilla 3DGS.

Approach. De4D-SLAM uses Gradient-Isolated Decoupling to leverage static residuals for motion segmentation with SA-KAN, plus Flow-Induced Initialization to stabilize 4D Gaussian optimization.

Problem. Reconstructing complete yet compact 3D Gaussian Splatting representations from sparse-view monocular video is challenged by correlated pseudo-views and repeated merging that accumulate structured, non-i.i.d. redundancy.

IncVGGT: Incremental VGGT for Memory-Bounded Long-Range 3D Reconstruction

Problem. Transformer-based 3D reconstruction with dense global attention becomes impractical for long sequences due to quadratic memory growth and excessive computation, even with streaming variants.
Approach. IncVGGT addresses this through two mechanisms: registering and fusing overlapping frames into composite views to reduce duplicate tokens, and history-side pruning retaining only top-k most relevant slots plus the most recent one.
Contribution. IncVGGT sustains arbitrarily long sequences on 10k-frame inputs under 80GB GPU, achieving 58.5x fewer operators and 25.7x less energy than StreamVGGT while maintaining comparable accuracy.

Approach. WEVSR adapts flow-matching video diffusion transformers via timestep sampling and noise augmentation for detail restoration, and introduces a lightweight multi-level discrete wavelet transform (DWT) front-end for the VAE encoder to inject frequency priors.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple VSR benchmarks without modifying the pretrained decoder.
diffusion modelswavelet transformvideo super-resolution
ICML 2026

ImmersePro: End-to-End Stereo Video Synthesis Via Implicit Disparity Learning

Problem. Generating high-quality stereo videos from single-view video sequences requires accurate disparity estimation and scale-aware synthesis.
Approach. ImmersePro uses a dual-branch architecture with spatial-temporal attention mechanisms employing implicit disparity guidance to generate stereo pairs without explicit disparity maps, along with a new large-scale YouTube-SBS dataset.
Contribution. ImmersePro improves stereo-from-mono baselines by 11.76% (L1), 6.39% (SSIM), and 5.10% (PSNR) on diverse video sequences.
stereo synthesisimplicit disparityvideo generation
ICML 2026

Bend the Basics: Degradation-Aware Deformable Tokenization for All-in-One Image Restoration

Problem. All-in-one image restoration seeks a single unified model for diverse spatially non-uniform corruptions, but fixed patch partitioning leaves embedding and reconstruction stages insensitive to local degradation variations.
Approach. FIT (Flexible Image Transformer) uses a Degradation Encoder to predict global degradation vectors and spatial maps, which jointly condition patch embedding and unembedding through adaptive deformation, plus task-token dropout for robustness.
Contribution. FIT achieves state-of-the-art performance on five standard benchmarks with 30.72 dB average PSNR on five-degradation settings, outperforming recent unified methods by 0.5-1.1 dB.
image restorationdegradation awarenessadaptive deformationtask-conditional learning
ICML 2026

RGGT: A Generative-Prior-Guided Transformer for Unified Rigid and Non-Rigid Point Cloud Registration

Problem. Point cloud registration faces distinct challenges in rigid vs non-rigid settings due to multi-scale discrepancy between global transformation consistency and local deformation, resisting unified approaches.
Approach. RGGT uses generative-prior-guided Transformer with coordinated design across representation, architecture, and supervision: generative priors enrich features, Global-Self-Cross Attention models multi-scale reasoning, dual objective provides consistent supervision.
Contribution. Achieves sota accuracy on rigid (ModelNet40, 3DMatch, KITTI) and non-rigid (4DMatch) benchmarks within single unified framework, demonstrating multi-scale geometric-semantic capture.
point cloud registrationgenerative modelsattention mechanismsgeometric learning
ICML 2026

Geometric Reciprocity: Unlocking Self-Supervision for Stereoscopic Video Generation

Problem. Monocular-to-stereo conversion requires stereo inpainting of disocclusions but training-based methods rely on scarce stereo pairs or synthetic data with domain gaps.
Approach. Proposes Geometric Reciprocity Theorem showing disocclusion mask when synthesizing target view equals mask of pixels lost warping from target to source, enabling self-supervised learning from monocular videos via cycle consistency.
Contribution. First self-supervised framework for monocular-to-stereo conversion achieving improvements over training-free and supervised state-of-the-art methods.
self-supervised learningstereo synthesisdepth estimationimage inpainting

LSGQuant: Layer-Sensitivity Guided Quantization for One-Step Diffusion Real-World Video Super-Resolution

Problem. One-step diffusion models for video super-resolution suffer from high computational cost and substantial model size, with naive quantization failing due to high dynamic range of latents.
Approach. LSGQuant estimates layer sensitivity via calibration statistics and implements Variance-Oriented Layer Training Strategy; introduces Dynamic Range Adaptive Quantizer and Quantization-Aware Optimization.
Contribution. Achieves nearly full-precision performance with significant speedup and significantly exceeds existing quantization techniques.
quantizationlayer sensitivitydiffusion models
ICML 2026

Difference-Aware Decision Learning for Multimodal Image Fusion

Problem. Multimodal image fusion suffers from cross-modal discrepancies and local conflicts creating uncertain modality allocation, causing information loss or artifact propagation.
Approach. Formulates fusion as observation-conditioned probabilistic decision-learning problem using cross-modal differences as decision triggers, constructing spatial and spectral decision conditions mapped to interpretable contribution policies.
Contribution. Demonstrates stable and competitive performance against state-of-the-art methods on multiple fusion tasks with uncertainty modulation for conservative mixing under insufficient evidence.
multimodal fusiondecision learningprobabilistic allocation
ICML 2026

Structure-Aware Riemannian Flow Matching for Registration and Fusion of Hyperspectral and Multispectral Images

Problem. Precise alignment of hyperspectral and multispectral images for fusion requires handling complex non-rigid deformations, yet existing methods suffer from inter-task error accumulation or neglect geometric distortions.
Approach. Proposes Structure-Aware Riemannian Flow Matching (SA-RFM) reformulating registration as dynamic optimal transport on a structure-induced Riemannian manifold via conditional flow matching with Riemannian objectives.
Contribution. Demonstrates superiority across four datasets with joint registration and fusion handling complex non-rigid deformations through geometry-informed framework.
optimal transportriemannian flow matchingimage registrationimage fusion
ICML 2026

MotionGRPO: Overcoming Low Intra-Group Diversity in GRPO-Based Egocentric Motion Recovery

Problem. Full-body 3D human motion recovery from head-mounted signals suffers from local joint reconstruction errors due to reliance on global distribution matching in diffusion-based methods.
Approach. MotionGRPO frames diffusion sampling as a Markov decision process optimized via Group Relative Policy Optimization, using a hybrid reward combining learned perceptual models and explicit joint constraints, with a noise-injection strategy to increase sample diversity.
Contribution. MotionGRPO achieves state-of-the-art performance in 3D motion recovery from egocentric sensors with improved visual fidelity and addresses the vanishing gradient problem from limited intra-group sample diversity.
reinforcement learningdiffusion modelsgroup relative policy optimizationreward shaping

LaRA-Fusion: Latent-Robust Adaptation via Dual-Loop Constraints for Infrared and Visible Image Fusion

Problem. Unsupervised infrared-visible image fusion overlooks intrinsic topological consistency across modalities; encoders succumb to degenerate shortcuts capturing noise rather than semantic structures.
Approach. LaRA-Fusion enforces dual-loop manifold constraints where an inner loop ensures geometric reversibility and an outer loop aligns representations with target distribution, mitigating latent space collapse.
Contribution. Extracts topologically aligned features robust to modality-specific variations, outperforming state-of-the-art methods with superior robustness and interpretability through latent-robust adaptation.

SMD: Multi-view Safety-Critical Driving Video Generation in the Real-world Domain

Problem. Safety-critical scenarios needed to test autonomous driving systems are rare, and existing generators only produce trajectories, simulations, or single-view videos rather than realistic multi-view video that modern AD systems actually consume.
Approach. SMD couples a safety-critical trajectory engine with a diffusion-based multi-view video generator; it uses a GRPO-fine-tuned VLM to select hazard-inducing vehicles, a two-stage process to generate collision-then-evasion trajectories, and a diffusion model to render these into multi-view videos.
Contribution. Generated videos substantially increase collision rates in stress tests and reduce collision rates when used in training, improving planner robustness.
diffusion modelstrajectory generationvision-language modelsreinforcement learning

Rays as Pixels: Learning A Joint Distribution of Video and Camera Trajectories

Problem. Recovering camera parameters and rendering scenes from sparse views involve separate forward and inverse problems that fail under sparse coverage or ambiguous poses.
Approach. Rays as Pixels learns a joint distribution of videos and camera trajectories using a specialized Video Diffusion Model with Decoupled Self-Cross Attention, representing cameras as dense ray pixels.

Contribution. Restores robust optimization throughout incremental learning, counteracting the cascading erosion of preservation gradients in magnitude, direction, and support coverage.

Problem. In-context localization for category-agnostic object localization is fragile and requires explicit category supervision, introducing bias toward semantic priors.
Approach. Introduces two-stage framework optimizing in-context attention between support boxes and query images without category supervision, refined via GRPO to minimize localization error.
Contribution. A 7B model trained with the method outperforms 72B models, demonstrating that context-aware localization objectives surpass scaling.
in-context learningvisual groundingpolicy optimization
ICML 2026

RefChess: Training-Free Contextual Search for Zero-Shot Referring Image Segmentation

Problem. Zero-shot referring image segmentation struggles with robust proposal selection because methods typically score proposals independently and get distracted by visually similar candidates.
Approach. RefChess uses a training-free contextual search framework that evaluates candidate masks under sampled distractor contexts via Monte-Carlo Tree Search, guided by a stability-aware reward integrating language decomposition and vision-language similarity.
Contribution. RefChess consistently improves robustness and referring segmentation performance on standard RIS benchmarks without task-specific training.

GUI-Spotlight: Adaptive Iterative Focus Refinement for Enhanced GUI Visual Grounding

Problem. Multimodal LLMs struggle with visual grounding—reliably mapping textual references to exact on-screen elements—limiting accurate pointer-level actions like clicking.

RSAgent: Learning to Reason and Act via Multi-Turn Tool Invocations for Text-Guided Segmentation

Problem. Text-guided object segmentation requires both cross-modal reasoning and precise pixel grounding, but single forward passes limit verification and refinement when initial localization is incorrect.
Approach. RSAgent is an agentic MLLM that interleaves reasoning and action through multi-turn tool invocations, revising pixel hypotheses iteratively based on visual feedback.

ICML 2026

DSGCR: Decomposed Spectral Geometry-Aware Cross-Modal Semantic Representation for 3D Visual Grounding

Problem. 3D visual grounding requires fine-grained semantic alignment and precise geometric reasoning, but unimodal encoders and handcrafted spatial priors fail to capture complex object relations.
Approach. Text-Aware Feature Tuning (TFT) injects linguistic context into visual hierarchy for early cross-modal alignment, while Decomposed Spectral Geometry (DSG) learns Fourier basis and decomposes pairwise relations into symmetric/antisymmetric spectral components.
Contribution. The approach achieves 2.05% Acc@0.25 improvement for 3DREC and 1.09% mIoU for 3DRES on ScanRefer through learnable spectral decomposition of spatial relations.
text-aware feature tuningspectral geometryfourier basis3d visual grounding
ICML 2026

Open-Text Aerial Detection: A Unified Framework For Aerial Visual Grounding And Detection

Problem. Existing methods for aerial scene understanding are split into two isolated paradigms: open-vocabulary detection handles category-level semantics but cannot localize multiple targets, while visual grounding handles single-target localization but lacks rich semantic understanding.
Approach. OTA-Det unifies both paradigms by reformulating the task to enable joint training across datasets with dense supervision, and introduces dense semantic alignment at multiple granularities to establish correspondence from holistic expressions to individual attributes.
Contribution. OTA-Det achieves state-of-the-art performance on six benchmarks spanning both open-vocabulary aerial detection and remote sensing visual grounding while maintaining real-time inference at 34 FPS.

MetaSpatial: Reinforcing 3D Spatial Reasoning in VLMs for the Metaverse

Problem. Vision-language models lack inherent 3D spatial reasoning to generate realistic scene layouts without post-processing, and supervised fine-tuning is inefficient due to scarcity of perfect annotations.

GRASP: Awakening Latent Spatial Reasoning in LVLMs via Training-free Geometric Rectification

Problem. Large Vision-Language Models exhibit remarkable capabilities but struggle significantly with spatial reasoning tasks due to representation-output misalignment where internal spatial encoding differs from final text output.
Approach. Proposes GRASP, a training-free framework using Manifold Differential Search to identify geometric counterfactuals, then applying dual-level rectification with Implicit Trajectory Correction for intermediate layers and Explicit Distribution Alignment for output.
Contribution. Achieves accuracy gains up to 26.1% on image spatial reasoning and 9.7% on video reasoning without parameter updates, consistently outperforming baselines across diverse architectures.

FSOD-VFM: Few-Shot Object Detection with Vision Foundation Models and Graph Diffusion

Problem. Few-shot object detection with vision foundation models suffers from bounding box overfragmentation, producing small false positives instead of complete detections.
Approach. FSOD-VFM integrates SAM2, DINOv2, and a universal proposal network, using graph-based diffusion to reweight box confidence scores across a network of predicted boxes for improved granularity.

ICML 2026

Looking Locally: Object-Centric Vision Transformers as Foundation Models for Efficient Segmentation

Problem. Current segmentation models encode entire images before focusing on specific objects, wasting computational resources on irrelevant regions.
Approach. FLIP (Fovea-Like Input Patching) selectively samples multi-resolution patches centered on objects of interest, allocating high-resolution processing to object centers while maintaining coarser peripheral context via biologically-inspired top-down attention.
Contribution. With 440× fewer parameters than SAM2-L (0.51M vs 224.45M), FLIP-Tiny reaches 79.90% mean IoU vs SAM2-L's 75.87%, and FLIP-Large achieves 83.26% IoU while running 2× faster than SAM2-L.

ICML 2026

Solving Spatial-Spectral Fusion with Latent Spectral Operators

Problem. Existing deep spatial–spectral fusion (SSF) methods typically learn the fusion mapping in the coordinate domain using convolutions and attentions, making it hard to scale across varying spatial resolutions and offering limited control over the frequency content of the reconstructions, which may further lead to severe spectral distortion. In this work, we propose Latent Spectral Operators (LSO), a SSF framework that learns fusion mappings between spectral functions through a structured operator parameterization.
Approach. Specifically, LSO first applies a cross-attention projection, where learned latent tokens serve as spectral prompts, to compress high-dimensional observations into a compact latent representation, and then adopts a hierarchical, patch-based architecture to integrate rich multi-scale cues. Furthermore, to parameterize the latent fusion operator in a controllable manner, a Trigonometric Basis Solver is elaborated, which represents the mapping using a trigonometric basis expansion.
Contribution. This formulation naturally supports multi-frequency modeling, with a capacity-stability trade-off governed by the number of basis functions. Extensive experiments on the CAVE and Harvard benchmarks demonstrate that LSO achieves consistent state-of-the-art performance and exhibits strong transferability across different spatial scales..
attention

Understanding Dataset Distillation via Spectral Filtering

Problem. Diverse dataset distillation methods exist but their underlying connections remain largely unexplored, limiting understanding of what distillation fundamentally achieves.
Approach. We introduce UniDD, a spectral filtering framework unifying diverse DD objectives by interpreting each as a filter applied to eigenvalues of the feature-feature correlation matrix.
Contribution. UniDD reveals that DD fundamentally matches frequency-specific features and proposes Curriculum Frequency Matching (CFM) that gradually adjusts filter parameters to cover both low- and high-frequency information.

Making models faster and more efficient 32

Compressing and optimizing vision models so they run on limited hardware without losing accuracy.

Feature Collapse Under Corruption: An Entropy Perspective on Robust Neural Networks

Problem. Neural networks remain vulnerable to natural corruptions despite extensive robustness research, with the underlying mechanisms of this vulnerability unclear even for modern transformers and foundation models.
Approach. The paper observes that natural corruptions collapse network feature spaces into high-entropy states; it proposes Dem-HEC, an entropy-guided fine-tuning framework that generates high-entropy samples and repairs models using both clean and corrupted examples, optionally combined with knowledge distillation from teacher snapshots.
Contribution. Dem-HEC improves corruption robustness across CNNs, transformers, and foundation models (including DinoV3) while maintaining or boosting clean accuracy on datasets from small to large resolution.

Approach. DispViT shifts to a regression-centric paradigm using a single-stream Vision Transformer that directly regresses disparity from tokenized binocular representations, with shift-embedding to encode disparity shifts and a lightweight refinement module.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art accuracy with strong resilience to matching ambiguities and wide disparity ranges by prioritizing holistic regression over explicit matching.

Problem. Large Vision-Language Models suffer from hallucination generating factually inaccurate content, and logit-level Contrastive Decoding mitigates this but compromises generation coherence, requiring restrictive penalty constraints unrelated to hallucination suppression.
Approach. Attention Contrastive Decoding (ACD) relocates part of contrastive operations to attention mechanism via Adaptive Subtraction Strategy (ASS) that attenuates hallucination-associated patterns while amplifying critical visual information.
Contribution. Combining ACD with existing CD methods produces substantially more coherent outputs with further reduced hallucinations, eliminating restrictive penalties while enabling trustworthy generation.
contrastive decodingattention mechanismshallucination mitigation
ICML 2026

Lottery Prior: Randomized Neural Compression for Zero-Shot Inverse Problems

Problem. Zero-shot inverse problems typically require highly complex models, but this intuition may not be necessary.
Approach. Lottery Prior combines lightweight neural network with entropy and complexity regularization in compression-based formulation, leveraging architectural priors from random networks with ensemble-based refinement.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art on denoising, noisy super-resolution, and inpainting with significantly fewer effective parameters; derives non-asymptotic error bounds for compression-based maximum-likelihood solvers.
neural compressionensemble methodsinverse problems

Contribution. Improves mAP@75 by up to 105% on rare-disease cases and surpasses adaptation baselines, with stronger calibration under distribution shifts.
